pii-bench-v3 — Ground Truth Viewer

Browse the pii-bench-v3 typed ground truth to audit label quality — the spans the benchmark claims are PII, over the real text. Ground truth only (no model). See how many of each type there are, then filter by PII type (audit one class at a time) and by dataset.

Two parts: (1) the ground-truth viewer below, and (2) a quality audit further down — where the labels come from (a systemic ai4privacy_300k offset bug) and likely labeling mistakes flagged heuristically + agentically.

v3 = pii-bench-v2 minus 11,780 non-PII spans (generic account-type descriptors mislabeled as Organization Name). The cards below are a stratified sample of the test split: 1,252 docs / 3,528 spans (rare types shown in full). The table is the full test split (135,283 spans / 51,363 docs).

Part 2 · ground-truth quality audit

Where the labels come from — and one broken link

Before auditing individual labels: most of pii-bench-v3’s ground truth is sound, but one subset — ai4privacy_300k — has a systemic offset bug that accounts for the large majority of raw flags (~15,597). Its char spans point a few characters past the entity they name, drifting further into each document. Tracing the provenance shows exactly where it entered:

1 · ai4privacy source
HF ai4privacy/pii-masking-300k — native privacy_mask offsets align to source_text 99.4% across all languages (English 99.2%, Spanish 99.9%, Dutch 100.0%, German 100.0%, Italian 98.8%, French 98.2%); JSON docs 402/402 (100%). correct
↓
2 · external benchmark
Pritesh-2711/pii-bench — stores mBERT tokens + BIO labels (no char offsets). For ai4privacy_300k only, EMAIL entities whose labeled token-run actually contains “@”: 44% (every other source 99–100%). The token↔label drift is introduced here. broken
↓
3 · our pii-bench-v3 (this benchmark)
s3://…/detection/pii-bench-v3/ — char spans derived from Pritesh’s BIO labels, so the drift carried through: ai4privacy_300k email spans land on a real email only 36% of the time. Our v2→v3 step (dropping 11,780 Org spans) didn’t touch offsets. inherited

Per-source health (full test split). ai4privacy_300k is the outlier; the token-derived NER sources are clean.

Sourcedocsspansclean*email-align†top languages

These are excluded from the label-quality audit below — they’re one data bug, not thousands of independent labeling errors. Fix: re-derive ai4privacy_300k spans from ai4privacy’s native privacy_mask instead of Pritesh’s BIO labels.

Likely labeling mistakes — clean sources

Candidate ground-truth errors on the clean sources (ai4privacy_300k excluded). Two methods: deterministic heuristics run over the full 100k-doc test split (exhaustive, high-precision — boundaries, formats, structure, cross-doc conflicts), and an agentic audit where 8 subagents judged an 80-doc stratified sample for the semantic categories heuristics can’t (57 findings). You asked for three categories (span boundary, wrong type, missing recall); we kept those and added five more:

Filter by mistake category

Span boundary — incomplete or too long requested 727 found · heuristic

The span exists but its edges are wrong — trailing/leading whitespace or punctuation, or it starts/ends mid-word (leading-token truncation).
